**COME CHECK OUT OUR NEW WEBSITE!!!  [www.mikesplants.com](http://www.mikesplants.com)**  
  
**WE SHIP ALL YEAR ROUND, ALWAYS OPEN FOR BUSINESS!**  
  
  
Below is an updated list/photos of Sarracenia divisions available for 2025.  This list supercedes all previous lists.  Plants are sent bare root.    
  
Small sized divisions should be flowering size within 2-3 years.  Medium sized division should be flowering size within a year.  Large plants are flowering sized rhizomes.     Shipping and handling typically ranges from as little as $11 up to around $40,  I always try to make it as reasonable as possible.  Average S&H is around $15-$17 per order.  Payment methods: Zelle, Venmo, cashapp, paypal, Cash, check, money orders.    If you need different payment instructions, please let me know, if I can accommodate, I will do what I can to work with you!     Please email orders  to **meizzwang@gmail.com**  before sending payment.  
   
If you're looking for anything not on this list, feel free to ask!  Anything you see on the internet or anything you can dream of (within reason, eg. it has to exist already) is fair game.  I always try my best to get back to you within 24 hours Monday thru Friday, and sometimes can get back to you quickly over the weekend as well!

**VENUS FLY TRAPS**  
D1 D. 'Royal Red': [farm1.staticflickr.com/761/21115126979_27676b993e_c.jpg](https://farm1.staticflickr.com/761/21115126979_27676b993e_c.jpg) one of the most popular red variants, very healthy plant available, inquire

 [c1.staticflickr.com/9/8241/29150907216_5049a55476_c.jpg](https://c1.staticflickr.com/9/8241/29150907216_5049a55476_c.jpg)   and [farm1.staticflickr.com/459/20302293798_d4a472c917_b.jpg](https://farm1.staticflickr.com/459/20302293798_d4a472c917_b.jpg)   large, $25   
D3 D. 'Big Mouth': [c7.staticflickr.com/9/8653/28696009486_346c7eeced_c.jpg](https://c7.staticflickr.com/9/8653/28696009486_346c7eeced_c.jpg)      big, fat plants available, much larger than you can get elsewhere, large, $20  
D4 D. 'B-52'-famous, vigorous with red traps that color up relatively easily.  Traps can get gigantic!   [live.staticflickr.com/65535/51397144270_3c609e98d2_c.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/51397144270_3c609e98d2_c.jpg)   large, $25, specimen sized, $35  
D5 D. 'Bohemian Garnet': [c1.staticflickr.com/9/8027/29080095392_694d800301_c.jpg](https://c1.staticflickr.com/9/8027/29080095392_694d800301_c.jpg)  large, healthy plants, $25  
D6 D. Holland Red- under-rated plant, greenish petioles and solid dark red trap much like red dragon, forms nice specimens over time,N/A  
D7 D. G-17 MEGATRAPS-incredible vigor, can form a large clump from a single bulb in one season!  Specimen sized plants produce absolutely enormous sized traps!  [live.staticflickr.com/65535/51398056328_22a567dcb8_c.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/51398056328_22a567dcb8_c.jpg)    large, $25 or specimen sized, $35  
D8 D. Orton's-exclusive, and available nowhere else!   These were wild seeds collected from the Orton's plantation back in the early 2000's.  Incredibly unique genetics, these produce some really big traps with very long petioles. This plant had traps so big, it was able to eat a baby lizard!  [c1.staticflickr.com/1/391/20165932382_b3b982f3ef_b.jpg](https://c1.staticflickr.com/1/391/20165932382_b3b982f3ef_b.jpg)   large, $25  
D9 D. 'Hosford'- from an infamous "invasive" population started by Old Man Northrop in the 1930's.  Likely represents genetics from wild populations that no longer exist.  Red colors in the interior of the trap seems to only show up in the center of the trap, whereas the "outer" portion is still green-very unique!   Not commonly available and hard to find.  Outstanding breeding potential.  Traps can get pretty large too under optimal conditions: [live.staticflickr.com/65535/48602555997_3c728277cf_c.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/48602555997_3c728277cf_c.jpg)    out of stock  
D10 D. muscipula Franklin Co, FL-Near impossible to acquire elsewhere, these are very rare genetics from a non-native population that is likely extinct in the Carolinas.  These grow fast and produce nice large traps, large, $20  
D11 D. muscipula Liberty Co, FL-vigorous and these produce very large traps!  Great breeding potential, nice red interior coloration.  Can produce big traps too!  Unique genetics, large, $20   
D12 D. muscipula G-14 rosette-this clone produces an abundance of really beautiful traps!  Doesn't get big, but is very productive and does stand out of the crowd!  [c3.staticflickr.com/9/8830/28696016706_42484aecaf_c.jpg](https://c3.staticflickr.com/9/8830/28696016706_42484aecaf_c.jpg)    large, $20  
D13 D. 'Colorado Giant'-a select clone that is very "teethy" and is one of the most vigorous clones in the collection!  From one bulb, it produced 10 plants in one season! Can produce very large sized traps!  [https://flic.kr/p/2mhKLWR](https://flic.kr/p/2mhKLWR)[Dionaea Colorado Giant](https://flic.kr/p/2mhKLWR) by [Mike Wang](https://www.flickr.com/photos/63000221@N05/), on Flickr  near specimen sized, $25   
D14 D. muscipula Onslow Co, NC-  **NEW** one of the only location data VFT's in the collection.  Unique genetics sought after by collectors and breeders, good diversity!  [live.staticflickr.com/65535/51386271775_7bbb431a7b_c.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/51386271775_7bbb431a7b_c.jpg)     Division from my mother plants, I have about 5 different genotypes,  med, $35

D18 D. Scarlatine- **NEW**  highly sought after variegated flytrap!  Lots of variation, even on the same plant!  [live.staticflickr.com/65535/52211133800_608f440f6a_c.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/52211133800_608f440f6a_c.jpg) [live.staticflickr.com/65535/52209640917_80d81e04a3_c.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/52209640917_80d81e04a3_c.jpg) [live.staticflickr.com/65535/52211133100_2b38c81636_c.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/52211133100_2b38c81636_c.jpg)  very limited,  small, $40  
D19 D. SD Draco- **NEW ** colors up easily even in less than optimal light!  great performer all season long, one of my favorites!  [live.staticflickr.com/65535/52358538865_8af9fb3702_z.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/52358538865_8af9fb3702_z.jpg)  med, $35  
D20 Dionaea GRUN-  **NEW **  anthocyanin free flytrap!  Can get a nice yellowish tinge under optimal conditions.  Vigorous and produces decent sized traps:https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/52197562569_bfd0bcb07e_z.jpg large, $20  
D21 D. muscipula Wolverine-  **NEW ** in my opinion, a better version of werewolf!  Surprisingly vigorous and easy to grow, so long as you start off with a strong, healthy plant!  [live.staticflickr.com/65535/52358116771_76f98f95f8_z.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/52358116771_76f98f95f8_z.jpg)  pretty amazing TEETH!https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/52358116571_b11636e661_z.jpg  med-small, super healthy plants, $30  
D22  D. Monkey Ass  **NEW ** -absolutely love the growth form of this clone!  Doesn't quite look like a buttox under my conditions, but still impressive: [live.staticflickr.com/65535/52358353653_ae13a02bf9_z.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/52358353653_ae13a02bf9_z.jpg)  med, $20  
D23  D. GIANT CLAM-  **NEW **  hard to come by and rare in cultvation!  Traps get really big, even on divisions that haven't yet reached vegetative maturity.  Very impressive clone: [live.staticflickr.com/65535/52245299427_e4942efe28_z.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/52245299427_e4942efe28_z.jpg) very limited stock,   large, $100  
D24 Dionaea Galaxy:  **NEW **   another hard to come by clone!  Very beautiful and unique mutant: [live.staticflickr.com/65535/52168804184_b306230b27_z.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/52168804184_b306230b27_z.jpg)  med-small, $60  
D25 D. Big tomato-  **NEW ** vigorous, easy to grow clone that forms nice clumps in a relatively short period of time.  I received a large plant one year before this pic was taken: [live.staticflickr.com/65535/52246559509_e7eeb37e71_z.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/52246559509_e7eeb37e71_z.jpg)  med, $20  
  
  
**DARLINGTONIA (COBRA LILY)**  Lots of new, exclusive offers!  Keep in mind, darlingtonia tends to double, if not triple in size right after the dormancy period.  Lots more expected to be available either June 2024 or September 2024, so stay tuned!      
  
DAR1 D. california 'alpine farms' Del Norte Co, CA: the most sought after darlingtonia variant in existence!   
 Solid red from head to toe under optimal conditions: [live.staticflickr.com/5350/10262670945_1555a0f8c3_c.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/5350/10262670945_1555a0f8c3_c.jpg)  Here's an alpine farms plant grown and photographed by Stefan Barth(?).  With the right conditions, they can color up even more amazingly in cultivation! [live.staticflickr.com/65535/53197248462_ce8b243899_c.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/53197248462_ce8b243899_c.jpg)  small,  $100  (note: these are on the small side but are in absolutely tip top shape with excellent roots and incredible growth momentum.  Expected to size up quickly with proper care.  
  
DAR2 D. california 'like alpine farms' Del Norte Co, CA: **NEW **from a population that is located very far away from the alpine farms site.  Genetically unique and different from the alpine farms variant.  Solid red from head to toe just like the alpine farms variant, except slightly different shape.  Extremely rare in nature and in cultivation: [live.staticflickr.com/2806/10848499096_1442b843cc_c.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/2806/10848499096_1442b843cc_c.jpg) limited  small, $100  
  
DAR3 D. californica 'regular form' Rattlesnake Creek, Josephine Co, OR- **NEW** from a location that is new to cultivation!    Unique population far away from the hot, lower elevation sites, this originates from a site that is very remote and there are no other seeps nearby!  Genetically unique population: [live.staticflickr.com/65535/51421386975_34d94ef55f_z.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/51421386975_34d94ef55f_z.jpg)  small, $35  
  
DAR4 D. californica 'coppery red' del Norte Co, CA-: **NEW**  incredible colors, usually colors up late fall/early winter: [live.staticflickr.com/65535/51420580818_d78297f01c_c.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/51420580818_d78297f01c_c.jpg)  [live.staticflickr.com/65535/51421295915_20fa2e63bb_z.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/51421295915_20fa2e63bb_z.jpg)  very limited, med-small, $90  
  
DAR5 D. californica Cedar springs, Del Norte Co, CA-very beautiful population, somewhat uniform: [sarracenia.proboards.com/thread/966/californica-cedar-springs-del-norte](https://sarracenia.proboards.com/thread/966/californica-cedar-springs-del-norte)  small, $35  
  
DAR6 D. californica 'dark purple' Del norte Co, CA- **NEW** a new, dark variant different from the Curry co, OR genetics!  this one is a chocolatey dark purple red color, never offered before! [live.staticflickr.com/7462/15523520759_0c6c481a9d_c.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/7462/15523520759_0c6c481a9d_c.jpg)  small, $160  
  
DAR7 D. californica 'red side' Del Norte Co, CA- **NEW** selected for the red coloration on the side of the head:https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/51420590233_c2df1cb7e4_c.jpg small, $35  
  
DAR8 D. californica the ponds Del Norte Co, CA-great genetic diversity, some red variants possible!  one of the most vigorous and easiest to grow populations in cultivation: [sarracenia.proboards.com/thread/970/californica-ponds-del-norte-ca](https://sarracenia.proboards.com/thread/970/californica-ponds-del-norte-ca) small, $35  
  
DAR9 D. californica 'Mountain creek' Curry Co, OR: **NEW**  genetically unique mountain variant, from a large population of super vigorous,  large trapped and good looking plants: [www.cpukforum.com/forum/index.php?/topic/54246-darlingtonia-in-the-wild-mountain-creek-curry-co-or/](https://www.cpukforum.com/forum/index.php?/topic/54246-darlingtonia-in-the-wild-mountain-creek-curry-co-or/)  small, $35  
  
DAR10 D. californica 'incredible' Josephine Co, OR: **NEW** inland population, where it gets really hot.  From some absolutely stunning plants, many red forms as well!  [live.staticflickr.com/65535/50481569157_c5dde14a39_c.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/50481569157_c5dde14a39_c.jpg)   small, $35  
  
DAR11 D. californica 'upper' (various clones)  Josephine Co, OR: inland population, where it gets very hot.   lots of diversity in shapes, color forms, and a high frequency of plants with extensive windows: [live.staticflickr.com/65535/50481567782_2d6e744d22_c.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/50481567782_2d6e744d22_c.jpg)   [live.staticflickr.com/65535/51421152124_989133d6c4_z.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/51421152124_989133d6c4_z.jpg) [live.staticflickr.com/65535/50481487581_9beb645564_c.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/50481487581_9beb645564_c.jpg)    small, $35  
  
DAR12 D. californica 'lower' (various clones)  Josephine Co, OR: inland population, where it gets very hot.  Only from gigantic plants!   small, $35  
  
DAR13 D. californica 'green body, red tongue'  rattlesnake seep Josephine Co, OR: **NEW** this is a unique population far away from the hot, inland populations and is high up in the mountains!  Nice extensive windows with most plants in this population, this particular selection has some stand out red tongues that contrast very well with the solid green bodies!  genetically unique:  [live.staticflickr.com/65535/51421166269_54f26abe1e_z.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/51421166269_54f26abe1e_z.jpg)  small, $40  
  
DAR14 D. californica 'giant bronze head, less red' Del Norte Co, CA: **NEW **this variant can produce enormous heads at vegetative maturity.  There is less red coloration on this one compared to other similar coppery headed types.  small, $80  
  
DAR15 D. californica 'dark red side, big heads' Del Norte Co, CA: **NEW** unique, uncommon coloration on the side of the trap.  very vigorous plants that are sizing up quicker than other populations.  way more red than the average clone, and it gets quite dark too!   most likely a giant form. [live.staticflickr.com/65535/51420596908_e8e9d17857_z.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/51420596908_e8e9d17857_z.jpg)  med-small, $70     
  
  
  
  
  
** Non-carnivorous  **   
1) Lilium catesbaei Southern Baldwin Co, AL: [https://farm6.staticflickr.com/5828/21114148498_6dbd421ed9_c.jpg](https://farm6.staticflickr.com/5828/21114148498_6dbd421ed9_c.jpg)[farm6.staticflickr.com/5828/21114148498_6dbd421ed9_c.jpg](https://farm6.staticflickr.com/5828/21114148498_6dbd421ed9_c.jpg)  Grow like a Sarracenia, except don't sit in water, tips and the full story can be found here: [http://sarracenia.proboards.com/thread/1524/lilium-catesbaei?page=3](http://sarracenia.proboards.com/thread/1524/lilium-catesbaei?page=3)[sarracenia.proboards.com/thread/1524/lilium-catesbaei?page=3](http://sarracenia.proboards.com/thread/1524/lilium-catesbaei?page=3)  near blooming sized bulbs (most of them should bloom after receiving cold winter dormancy), $20/each   
  
1A Lilium catesbaei Lamar Co, MS: first time entering cultivation from this locality! about a year from flowering sized, $20/each  
  
1b Lilium iridollae-  **NEW **  grow just like L. catesbaei, except place them either in part shade or have them surrounded by very tall plants if growing in full sun.  They seem to burn in blasting full sun: [sarracenia.proboards.com/thread/4931/lilium-iridollae-threatened-lily](https://sarracenia.proboards.com/thread/4931/lilium-iridollae-threatened-lily)  sold out inquire around September 2025, hoping to have more then  
  
  
2) Iris verna Onslow Co, NC-not all I. verna are the same, this is an exceptional clone: [sarracenia.proboards.com/thread/4085/iris-verna-jaw-dropping-flower](http://sarracenia.proboards.com/thread/4085/iris-verna-jaw-dropping-flower)  exact clone pictured in the thread with multiple growth points, $25  
  
3) Iris verna var. smalliana Cobb Co, GA-an absolute stunner clone with neon purple flowers and a relatively big, orange center!   very easy to grow, vigorous, and forms nice clumps over time.   [live.staticflickr.com/65535/51315095762_c1b6f89078_c.jpg](https://live.staticflickr.com/65535/51315095762_c1b6f89078_c.jpg) Extremely rare, select clone that is just making its way into circulation.  1-2 growth points, $35/each  
  
4) Iris verna var. smalliana SE Bartow Co, GA:  **NEW!**  another exceptional clone: [sarracenia.proboards.com/thread/5820/iris-verna-var-smalliana-bartow](https://sarracenia.proboards.com/thread/5820/iris-verna-var-smalliana-bartow)  $35 1-2 growth points, great healthy plants!  
  
3) Sabatia dodecandra-outstandng bog companion plant!  Perennial that comes back year after year.  Profusion of brilliant pink flowers.  Inquire  
  
5) Wasabia japonica 'Daruma'  Yes, the real deal WASABI!!!  Most people have never eaten real wasabi: sushi restaurants typically use horse radish with food coloring!    Takes about 2 years before you can harvest the rhizome to make wasabi, but the delicious leaves can be stir-fried in the meantime!   These are established divisions and they should take off if kept in good conditions!   Keep in PURE SHADE and keep the plants cool.   Leaves will wilt in direct sunlight if the temps are above 50F.    Feed lightly 1-2x a month when temps are between 45F-74F.  Use good quality water (ie. CP water).  They like growing in 50/50 washed sand/potting soil or grow in a well amended shady spot outside with ferns, rhododendrons, or other shade lovers.  [https://farm5.staticflickr.com/4609/25104362927_63d32f376f_c.jpg](https://farm5.staticflickr.com/4609/25104362927_63d32f376f_c.jpg)[farm5.staticflickr.com/4609/25104362927_63d32f376f_c.jpg](https://farm5.staticflickr.com/4609/25104362927_63d32f376f_c.jpg) out of stock  
  
6) Wasabia japonica  'Mazuma'-another cultivar of the real deal Wasabi!  Most people have never eaten real wasabi: Sushi restaurants typically use horse radish with food coloring!   This clone seems to be a bit more heat tolerant than Daruma.  Takes about 2 years to harvest the rhizome to make wasabi, but the delicious leaves can be stir-fried in the meantime!  These are established divisions and they should take off if kept in good conditions!   Keep in PURE SHADE and keep the plants cool.   Leaves will wilt in direct sunlight if the temps are above 50F.    Feed lightly 1-2x a month when temps are between 45F-74F.  They like growing in 50/50 washed sand/potting soil or grow in a well amended shady spot outside with ferns, rhododendrons, or other shade lovers.  Use good quality water. (ie. CP water) [https://farm5.staticflickr.com/4674/39076562435_ab60993a10_c.jpg](https://farm5.staticflickr.com/4674/39076562435_ab60993a10_c.jpg)[farm5.staticflickr.com/4674/39076562435_ab60993a10_c.jpg](https://farm5.staticflickr.com/4674/39076562435_ab60993a10_c.jpg)   Regular sized division, harvestable in 1 year if grown optimally, leaves mostly cut off, $30    
  
  
  
**BOG ORCHIDS  NEW**  
  
OR-1 Pogonia ophioglossoides Florida: super vigorous, produces amazingly dense matts of flowers over time  can be shipped all year round, since we carry established divisions which will be sent fully in tact (bare root). #### **Mike Wang** (`@meizzwang`)
`post #12` · `id=2154` · `2013-03-13 15:52:11 UTC`

HI David,  
  
Here's the story behind the answer to your inquiry. I cross my two best flava rugelii clones, AxB, and grew out a huge seed batch. Out of 100's of plants, 2 of them grew faster than all the others, had shapely pitchers, and a very well defined red splotch. I labeled them AxB clone #1 and AxB clone #2   
  
If you're looking for well shaped pitchers, I'd go with AxB clone #1. If you're looking for extremely fast growth rates, I'd go with AxB clone #2

Yes, getting my thired order in the next few weeks, i got a S. oreophila "alabama" (huge, grew a fall flower after i got it), and got a S. pururea subsp. venosa old dock (big plant, nicely colored), anf also got a S. jonessii "sherdian's clone x AF" as a freebie (in july 2013), Got a S. oreophila "sand hill mt. clone b" (it was bright red, had a nice sized rhizome), a S. purpurea subsp. venosa from burnswick Co XL (biggest purp i have ever seen in my life, looked like a huge head of red and purple cabbage), and a S. purpurea var. montana F2 (colorful cute little purp, will grow into a nice plant in a year or so, it had like 5 growing points), and got a S. psitticnia bay, Co FL(freebie). #### *theplantman*
`post #105` · `id=21051` · `2015-08-18 18:11:57 UTC`
*quotes posts: 21046*

![ErrorEN Avatar](//storage.proboards.com/3292456/avatar/UdZE5516WYeVktKhblmf.jpg) [not mirrored]

[Aug 18, 2015 12:17:24 GMT -5](/post/21046/thread) [ErrorEN](/user/401) said:

Hey [meizzwang](/user/11) would you happen to have any 'Judith Hindle' for sale? Sad to say I lost mine a few years ago and never bothered replacing it because I thought it was "common". (Yes I was one of *those* people. 

![;)](//storage.proboards.com/forum/images/smiley/wink.png) [not mirrored]

) 

I overlooked Judy for years because I thought the same. But honestly, now I'm almost as attracted to 'JH' as 'AS.' And furthermore, 'JH' will create some insanely cool hybrids, especially regarding lid size and coloration. The flowers are pretty attractive, too.  
  
If 'JH' **weren't **common, it'd be insanely high-priority on my want list. I'm trying less and less to evaluate plants based on supply and demand. Rather, it's much better to be aesthetically pleased with a plant--and own it for that reason--than to simply own something because it's rare.  
  
My favorite part is the early pitcher coloration. Now tell me: if you saw something like this, without knowing what this plant was, what would your reaction be? Pretend it wasn't even 'Judith Hindle'--let's say it was a newly listed cultivar in CPN. I know that I'd be floored and go after a chunk of this sucker with a vengeance!!  
  
[http://s960.photobucket.com/user/kevintheplantman/media/Carnivorous%20Plants/IMG_6572_zpsf2b32edd.jpg.html](http://s960.photobucket.com/user/kevintheplantman/media/Carnivorous%20Plants/IMG_6572_zpsf2b32edd.jpg.html)

[Aug 18, 2015 12:17:24 GMT -5](/post/21046/thread) [ErrorEN](/user/401) said:

Hey [meizzwang](/user/11) would you happen to have any 'Judith Hindle' for sale? Sad to say I lost mine a few years ago and never bothered replacing it because I thought it was "common". (Yes I was one of *those* people. 

![;)](//storage.proboards.com/forum/images/smiley/wink.png) [not mirrored]

) 

I overlooked Judy for years because I thought the same. But honestly, now I'm almost as attracted to 'JH' as 'AS.' And furthermore, 'JH' will create some insanely cool hybrids, especially regarding lid size and coloration. The flowers are pretty attractive, too.  
  
If 'JH' **weren't **common, it'd be insanely high-priority on my want list. I'm trying less and less to evaluate plants based on supply and demand. Rather, it's much better to be aesthetically pleased with a plant--and own it for that reason--than to simply own something because it's rare.  
  
My favorite part is the early pitcher coloration. Now tell me: if you saw something like this, without knowing what this plant was, what would your reaction be? Pretend it wasn't even 'Judith Hindle'--let's say it was a newly listed cultivar in CPN. I know that I'd be floored and go after a chunk of this sucker with a vengeance!!  
  
[http://s960.photobucket.com/user/kevintheplantman/media/Carnivorous%20Plants/IMG_6572_zpsf2b32edd.jpg.html](http://s960.photobucket.com/user/kevintheplantman/media/Carnivorous%20Plants/IMG_6572_zpsf2b32edd.jpg.html)  
  

  
  
When AG3 stopped production of Judith Hindle we immediately held on to all we had to get plants big for divisions to reach a certain critical mass of sustainability with them.  I contend that they are the gold standard for a Sarracenia hybrid.  They tend to have it all; strikingly beautiful, medium height and stature, almost never floppy, good looking pitchers in early and late season, good looking flowers, and leaves that last well into the winter, and grows fast enough to get divisions from it. It's also a reasonably good bug catcher.  I've found that when I occasionally have large plants for sale and bring them down to Portland Saturday Market, I can just about name the price just because they are so beautiful.

#### *adaetz100*
`post #171` · `id=41281` · `2019-07-16 05:08:38 UTC`

I've been buying Sarracenias from Mike for years now, and he never ceases to amaze. As everyone who's bought from him will affirm, his plants are packaged with care and arrive promptly, and they are ALWAYS healthy, colorful, and quick to acclimate--with absolutely bulletproof roots. In fact, one of the plants that I received earlier this year has essentially doubled in size/pitcher height since I got it--and it's a leucophylla, so I'm eager to see what the late season holds... Additionally, every order I've received has contained at least one freebie plant, which is always a nice surprise to unpack (Mike picks 'em well).  
  
Aside from this, however, it's Mike's customer service and professionalism that keep me coming back. He always answers questions with patience and practical knowledge, and if he doesn't have the particular plant I'm searching for at a given time, he'll provide a generous handful of alternatives (he does the same thing for pricey plants, providing some more wallet-friendly options to mull over). With that said, I have been able to acquire specific 'wish list' plants from Mike (on more than a few occasions!) that really aren't available anywhere else, and I can trust that I'm getting the real deal even with rarer and more frequently imitated cultivars.  
  
If you've never ordered from Mike, feel free to start small--but it's extremely likely you'll become a regular like I have!  
  
Take care, everyone!  
-Andrew  
  
  
Attached are two pictures (if I can attach them correctly on my first post... fingers crossed) of the S. 'Waccamaw' I purchased from Mike back in early 2017. The first picture is actually the very first pitcher that opened after I got the plant--as you can tell by the color, the plant was extremely healthy and adjusted VERY quickly to my microclimate. The second picture is the resulting clump this June, after two years in my care (now with four flowers instead of one the first year). For an atropurpurea, this thing is darn vigorous, and I have no idea where else it would have been available.

In my never ending quest to improve the quality of my sarracenia collection ... I know I can count on Mike to have some stuff you just can't get anywhere else. Every time I inquire about plants, Mike always sends me a list of similar plants that aren't on the sales list in addition to what I inquired about. So ... anyone on the fence? Send Mike a quick email ... he'll hook you up. IE. There's way more to be had besides what's on his sales list ... just sayin.  
  
Years ago Mike sent me a flava 'Bronze'. I didn't know it was flava 'Bronze' because I'd ordered flava ornata 'Gulf Coast, FL. I wondered for years why it looked so different than everyone else's. After showing pics on this forum we came to the realization that indeed there was an unintentional mix up. Long story short ... 4 years later ... with my most recent order a couple of weeks ago, Mike sent me a free flava ornata 'Gulf Coast', FL. In no way did I expect this, as the 'Bronze' is a much more expensive specimen. Wow! Lifetime warranty on the identification of goods sold.

He gave me some amazing plants, some with a multitude of growth points that I honestly could've divided if I wanted to. He sent me an extra plant that is just vibrant. Honestly, it looks nicer than the plants I actually bought lol. I don't have the most sophisticated taste with Sarracenia but I've expanded my horizons just from the extra plants I've received.

#### *[guest] Andrew Marshall*
`post #281` · `id=46922` · `2021-08-21 19:46:06 UTC`

As a former dealer in carnivorous plants for many years (Cascade Carnivorous Plants, 1990-2005) and as a long time collector and grower, I have experience in recognizing really well grown plants and superb customer service stemming from experience and in-depth knowledge of the plants themselves.  I have dealt with Mike occasionally for years now, and my most recent purchase continues to show me that, while there are many people out there who grow carnivorous plants and many who sell them, there are very few people who have both the long-term experience and give the consistently great service that Mike provides.  He is always generous to a fault with his knowledge and his plants, and this puts him in with the best grower/dealers out there.  You may find them cheaper elsewhere occasionally, but you won't find many people like Mike out there, and his plants are always superb.  It has been, as always, a pleasure doing business with you Mike.

#### *planty*
`post #307` · `id=48257` · `2022-05-23 05:32:46 UTC`

Wow! Wow! Wow!🤩  
It was like Christmas Day when Mike Wang’s  plants arrived here yesterday!🏆🌟🥰🏆🌟🥰 👏   
I am certainly appreciating the Hurricane White Cone F, a dream plant for me! 🌅🌟I also purchased other fantastic Sarracenias and also great flytraps from Mike in 3 different orders in the past 6 months.  
This is the thing - I ordered many different cultivars of flytraps from 8 other vendors (7 vendors are well known, reputable carnivorous plant companies online, and I purchased several more flytraps from a person in our carnivorous plant society)  in November 2021 - March 2022. You would think that living in Southern CA the traps would be fine outdoors in our mild weather. Yet, all died EXCEPT the ones I got from Mike. So, obviously I’m a customer for life! Mike’s simply are better hardened off and stronger - they aren’t babied in temperature control greenhouses. So, you get plants that acclimate better to any environment because they deal with change better. Interestingly enough, the carnivorous plant society flytraps were raised outside and still died so there is something really special about Mike’s plants.  
The sizes of the plants are excellent, and they are packed well. I noticed that the roots on Sarracenias and flytraps are more developed and longer than many other companies which allows them to reach the water they need better.   
Also, Mike throws in a little present with each order - and every time it has been an awesome surprise!🤩  
Finally, Mike is a conscientious vendor. He sends emails telling you when a package is sent with a tracking number. He answers questions about carnivorous plants in emails. He treats people respectfully and kindly. I appreciate that.🥇

#### **Mike Wang** (`@meizzwang`)
`post #368` · `id=50266` · `2024-01-05 17:16:08 UTC`
*quotes posts: 50228*

![andynorth Avatar](//storage.proboards.com/forum/images/defaultavatar.png) [not mirrored]

[Dec 18, 2023 13:29:34 GMT -5](/post/50228/thread) [andynorth](/user/2372) said:

I understand Mike Wang's facility is in Oregon. Is it open to the public and if so, where about is it at?

  
  
Hi Andynorth!  We're located in Northern California!  We operate from home and live in a R-3 (rural residential) zoning, which unfortunately  prohibits us from having walk in traffic.  The reasoning is neighbors don't want an influx of strangers in their neighborhood and they also don't want gigantic greenhouse operations with beaming lights keeping them up all night long.  Most greenhouse operations also spray heavily with very dangerous pesticides which can severely contaminate the ground water that everyone out here relies on: we strictly grow everything outdoors and  don't use  ANY pesticides for that exact reason!      I reluctantly understand the reasoning, it's the price we pay to live here.      
  
That said, our plants are very easy to obtain  online and I will spend the time with anyone who has questions, wants to see pictures, or needs help with growing. Always feel free to reach out to me at meizzwang@gmail.com, I can put together a virtual tour of the collection for you via pictures!  
-Mike

#### *berberis*
`post #389` · `id=51659` · `2025-11-17 01:19:32 UTC`

I’ve ordered from Mike half a dozen times over the last year, and I’ve always been super happy with my experience. There are three things that make me a returning customer:  
  
1) plant quality and diversity. Mikes plants literally never disappoint, both in terms of the interesting genetics and how well they are grown. His plants look better coming out of a shipping box than I can seem to achieve in situ, and that’s saying a lot given the rigors of travel!  
  
2) customer service. Mike is always, always on top of his game. Super prompt and helpful; I’ve learned a lot from him for sure.   
  
3) freebies! Every order I’ve made has come with a gift plant, and they’re super nice plants, not little token presents at all. I’ve sometimes found I like these plants as much or more than the ones I ordered, so it’s been a great way to explore new species/localities.   
  
Anyway, I can’t recommend Mike highly enough. Absolutely a gold-standard seller.

#### *dannyd*
`post #393` · `id=51791` · `2026-02-09 00:21:58 UTC`

I was a Sarracenia collector many years back before taking a break due to several cross-country moves.  I recently got back into the hobby a year ago, and some of the first plants on my list were some of the most difficult to source.  My thorough searches led me to this forum and, consistently, Mike Wang’s listing.  I was nervous about sending a large sum of money to a person over the internet, but wow, did I end up happy.  Mike’s plants were amazing and far exceeded what was described.  As a trained plant pathologist, I could not believe how incredibly healthy the plants, and especially root systems were.  Packing and shipping were thoughtfully done.  Mike goes above and beyond to make his customers happy, and you can tell that he really loves what he does.  Not only would I buy again, but Mike remains my top source for all Sarracenia (except, unfortunately, the endangered species).
